34:LIGHT says that n nonnegative weights on an n-cycle, n>=2f, admit an independent f-set of weight<=f/n times
35:the total, also for a disjoint union of paths. Both complete used proofs/own scopes travel in the packet.
36:The RP-LIFT THEOREM, its R=p FWIN/ILIFT/SIGN, RP2NE's theorem/SIGN-2Z and DELTA1 lemmas are NOT premises.
37:The new CRT window, integer lift and all sign steps below are proved afresh. No CJ/TRANSFER/S(p)/Lev/CT,
38:HK1/HIGH-CORE, PartII, ARC+/SEG or machine check is used by THIS new proof.
39:
40:## 1. Avoider data at the actual R=p+2 domain
41:
42:Assume u avoids. Normalise c=1,A>=B by the accepted order-preserving group automorphisms. Write
43:u=R_1S_1...R_kS_k, R_i preceding S_i, l_i=|S_i| odd, a_i/b_i the positive/negative rotation counts,
44:r_i=a_i+b_i, d_i=a_i-b_i, theta_i=y_(i-1)-y_i in Z_p, u_i=theta_i/2. Indices are cyclic and empty runs genuine.
45:Then F=3p-4, H=p-3 and k=F-2H=p+2=R. Z0 gives 0<=B<=2. TheoremP/P0 gives
46: F+sum floor(r_i/2)<=3p-2, hence sum floor(r_i/2)<=2.                                       (1)
47:Let q be the number of nonempty runs, e=k-q the number of empty runs, v the number of nonempty EVEN runs.
48:Since R=k,
49: e=R-q=sum_(nonempty i)(r_i-1)>=v, and v<=2 by(1).                                        (2)
50:No run purity is assumed; every odd nonempty r_i has odd d_i, including mixed runs.
51:
52:## 2. Fresh fixed window and CRT equivalence
53:
54:Put M=p-2, L=3, U=(p-1)/2. For every integer m in[L,U],2m<=p-1<k and CONNECT applies.
55:For EVERY integer E in[0,R], not merely E<=p,
56: gamma_m(E)=min(p-m,E-kappa+m-1,floor(E/2))=floor(E/2):
57: E-kappa+m-1=E+m-3>=E>=floor(E/2), and p-m>=(p+1)/2=floor(R/2)>=floor(E/2).
58:Thus every proper nonempty pattern P with L<=m(P)<=U has the SAME floor-type W interval
59: I(P)=[-floor((R-E(P))/2),floor(E(P)/2)], E(P)=B+sum_(i in P)d_i in[0,R].                    (3)
60:
61:Let C=Z/(2p)Z and let e0:Z_p->C be the UNIQUE EVEN lift, explicitly e0(t)=(p+1)t modulo2p.
62:It is well-defined/additive because p+1 is even and p(p+1) is divisible by2p. Define
63: alpha_i=e0(theta_i)-d_i, psi(P)=-B+sum_(i in P)alpha_i=e0(2u_P)-E(P) in C.
64:Since sum theta_i=0 and D=A-B,
65: sum alpha_i=-D in C.                                                                    (4)
66:The W condition u_P in I(P) modp is EQUIVALENT to psi(P) having an integer representative in[-R,0].
67:Indeed the forward condition gives even t=2j in[-(R-E),E], with class e0(2u_P); then t-E is such a
68:representative. Conversely if s in[-R,0] represents psi, then t=s+E represents the even class e0(2u_P),
69:so t is even, lies in[-(R-E),E], and t/2 belongs to I(P) and is congruent to u_P modulo p.
70:R=p+2<2p, so the classes outside[-R,0] are exactly the integers[1,2p-R-1]=[1,p-3]=[1,M-1].
71:Avoidance therefore gives a unique representative
72: N(P) in[1,p-3] for EVERY P in each F_m, L<=m<=U.                                          (5)
73:
74:## 3. Fresh integer increments; all parity and size information
75:
76:An edge adding index i changes N by an integer representing alpha_i and lying in[-(p-4),p-4]. This
77:interval has2p-7<2p integers, so at most one represents alpha_i. Every index has an adding edge by CONNECT
78:(orient its toggling edge the other way if necessary). Call its unique integer h_i. The same bound and
79:congruence apply on ALL layers m in[L,U]; all adding edges have increment h_i, all removing edges -h_i.
80:CONNECT yields a constant c_m on each layer with
81: N(P)=c_m+sum_(i in P)h_i. No equality of c_m across layers is asserted or needed.
82:P and P^c lie on the same layer, hence(5) gives
83: abs(sum_(i in P)h_i-sum_(i notin P)h_i)<=p-4 for EVERY 3<=m(P)<=U.                         (6)
84:Also h_i represents alpha_i, |h_i|<=p-4, and
85: sum h_i=-D modulo2p.                                                                    (7)
86:
87:Each h_i has parity d_i, hence parity r_i. An empty run has theta_i!=0, so its h_i is a NONZERO EVEN
88:integer with |h_i|>=2. An odd run has odd nonzero h_i. A zero h_i must belong to a nonempty even run.
89:Let z be the number of zeros, E0 the empty-run set(|E0|=e), and Z0 the zero set(|Z0|=z). Then
90: e>=v>=z, z<=2; E0 and Z0 are disjoint. Put w_i=|h_i| and S=sum w_i.
91:Pointwise w_i>=1+1_(i in E0)-1_(i in Z0); the additional v-z nonzero even runs contribute at least one more.
92:Consequently
93: S>=k+e+v-2z>=k=p+2.                                                                     (8)
94:S is ODD because sum|h_i|=sum h_i=sum d_i=R modulo2.                                        (9)
95:If S=k, equality in(8) forces e=v=z; every odd nonempty run has r_i=1, every even nonempty run r_i=2
96:(by(2)), odd-run labels have magnitude1, empty labels magnitude2, even nonempty labels are0. In particular
97:ALL weights are<=2 in this equality case.                                                   (10)
98:This equality structure is derived, not a unit-label assumption on the original target.
99:
100:## 4. New LOW sign lemma, including p7
101:
102:Claim. Unless ALL nonzero h_i have one sign AND S=k, there is a sign vector s_i in{+1,-1} with exactly2m
103:cyclic changes for some3<=m<=U and sum s_i h_i>p-4, contradicting(6).
104:
105:Start with s_i=sign(h_i) on nonzero labels and either sign at zeros; the signed sum is S. Let2m0 be its
106:cyclic change count (m0=0 for one sign throughout). If the nonzero labels have both signs then EVERY
107:assignment at zeros has m0>=1. We prove all cases; in the remaining one-sign case take the same sign at zeros.
108:
109:A. 3<=m0<=U. The starting signs work: S>=p+2>p-4.
110:
111:B. m0>U. Since k=p+2 is odd, m0<=floor(k/2)=(p+1)/2=U+1, so m0=U+1. Among its2m0 nonempty constant-sign
112:stretches choose one of weight<=S/(2m0). Flip that whole stretch. Its two neighbours had the opposite sign,
113:so exactly two boundaries disappear, leaving2U changes (2m0>=8). The new sum is at least
114: S(1-1/m0)=S U/(U+1)>p-4,
115:since S>=p+2 and (p+2)(p-1)/(p+1)=p-2/(p+1)>p-4. Zero-weight stretches cause no problem.
116:
117:C. m0=1 or2, except temporarily p7,m0=2. Put f=3-m0. Let J be the union of the interiors of the2m0
118:constant-sign stretches. Each stretch excludes at most two endpoints, so n=|J|>=k-4m0.
119:If p>=11,m0=1 then n>=p-2>=9>2f=4; if p>=11,m0=2 then n>=p-6>=5>2f=2;
120:if p7,m0=1 then n>=5>4. Thus gamma=2f/n<1 in every current case.
121:LIGHT on these disjoint paths supplies an independent f-set Phi in J with weight<=f/n times W_J.
122:Original-cycle adjacency between two interior vertices lies in one such path: stretch endpoints are
123:excluded. Flipping Phi therefore adds EXACTLY2f boundaries, giving6=2L changes.
124:Write e_J=|E0 intersect J|,z_J=|Z0 intersect J|. The new signed sum is at least
125: W_(J^c)+(1-gamma)W_J
126: >= k+e-z-2f-gamma e_J+gamma z_J
127: >= k-2f-z+(1-gamma)e
128: =p-4+2m0-z+(1-gamma)e >p-4.                                                             (11)
129:Strictness: m0=2 gives2m0-z>=2; m0=1,z<=1 gives2m0-z>=1; m0=1,z2 gives e>=2 and1-gamma>0.
130:No e>z premise is assumed. The nonnegative-weight lower bounds are valid because1-gamma>0.
131:
132:D. p7,m0=2 (the missing small-prime case). Four stretches cover9 vertices, so one has length>=3, yielding
133:an interior vertex. Flip that single vertex, adding two boundaries and obtaining6 changes.
134:If S>9, oddness(9) gives S>=11 and its weight<=|h_i|<=p-4=3, so the new sum>=11-6=5>3.
135:If S9, equality structure(10) gives weight<=2, so the new sum>=9-4=5>3. No LIGHT assertion with n1 is used.
136:
137:E. m0=0. LIGHT on the full k-cycle with f3 (k>=9>=6) gives an independent three-set of weight<=3S/k.
138:Flipping it gives exactly6 changes and signed sum>=S(1-6/k)=S(p-4)/k. This is>p-4 when S>k.
139:If S=k this is the sole unexcluded case, with all nonzero h_i of one sign. The claim is proved. []
140:
141:The positive set P={i:s_i=+1} of the obtained signs is proper nonempty with m(P)=m; its signed sum is the
142:left expression in(6), so(6) is contradicted. We are left only with the one-sign S=k case of(10).
143:
144:## 5. The one-sign minimum case: B0/1/2 are ALL excluded
145:
146:Here e=v=z<=2, all odd nonempty runs are singletons, all even nonempty runs have2 letters/label0, and
147:all nonzero h_i have one sign. Since S=k=p+2, their total is either +(p+2) or -(p+2).
148:Equation(7), with D=p+2-2B and 0<=B<=2, implies:
149:* positive total: sum h_i+D=2p+4-2B is divisible by2p ONLY if B2;
150:* negative total: sum h_i+D=-2B is divisible by2p ONLY if B0.
151:Thus B1 is impossible; B2 forces the positive sign, B0 the negative sign. These are exact congruences,
152:not an imported R1 label classification. There is at least one positive singleton run: A=R-B>=p,
153:at most2v<=4 rotation letters lie in the even runs, so at least p-4>=3 positive letters lie in singleton runs.
154:
155:B2, all positive labels. Choose such a positive singleton i. Then d_i=1,h_i=1, so
156: e0(theta_i)=h_i+d_i=2 modulo2p; theta_i=2 modulo p and u_i=1.
157:For P={i}, m1,E(P)=B+d_i=3,E-=R-3=p-1. At kappa2,
158: gamma_1(3)=min(p-1,1,1)=1,
159: gamma_1(p-1)=min(p-1,p-3,(p-1)/2)=(p-1)/2.
160:The W interval[-(p-1)/2,1] contains u_P=1. This gives the forbidden identity2p-subword.
161:
162:B0, all negative labels. Every singleton has d_i=1,h_i=-1, so theta_i=0,u_i=0.
163:If e>=1, choose an empty index j. Its h_j=-2,d_j=0 imply theta_j=-2,u_j=-1.
164:There are k-2v>=p-2>=5 positive singleton runs. At most two are cyclic neighbours of j, so choose a
165:nonadjacent singleton i. P={i,j} has exactly m2 intervals, E(P)=1,E-=p+1 and u_P=-1. Directly,
166: gamma_2(1)=min(p-2,0,0)=0,
167: gamma_2(p+1)=min(p-2,p,(p+1)/2)=(p+1)/2.
168:Its W interval[-(p+1)/2,0] contains -1, again forbidden.
169:If e0, then v=z0, every rotation run is a singleton and theta_i=0 for EVERY i. All reflection-block
170:values y_i are equal, hence all F=3p-4>=2p reflection letters are one sigma_y. Any2p of them have product
171:sigma_y^(2p)=1 in original index order. This is also forbidden.
172:All exceptional cases are excluded; the assumption that u avoids is impossible. This proves D2-LOW. []
173:
174:## 6. Scope and process boundary
175:
176:The fixed window was proved for the ACTUAL R=p+2 and EVERY E in[0,p+2]; the singleton/two-index W tests
177:in Section5 are outside the fixed layers and were separately evaluated, not assumed to have the same window.
178:The m0=2,p7 sign step is explicit. No neutral-even count was silently made strict. No new proof assumes
179:unit labels, same-sign labels, run purity, B<kappa or nonempty gaps; the exceptional shape was derived only
180:from equality in the necessary count. Increasing-original-index order follows throughout from W and cyclic
181:conjugation: a selected product UV=1 remains VU=U^(-1)(UV)U=1 across the restored cut. Normalisation maps
182:identity products bijectively and never changes the selected index order.
183:

185:values require the separate arc-cover proof/baseline union. Parent alone controls reviews and acceptance.

187:model/native/network/nested agent or old-file mutation. The1674s checkpoint gap remains a474s target miss
188:below1800, not erased by a delayed intermediate clock. No novelty/preregistration/corroboration claim.

49:## 1. Actual R2 avoider data and pair capacity
50:
51:Assume u in C2 avoids. Normalise c=1,A>=B by the order-preserving automorphisms; F=3p-4>=1.
52:Put h=(p+1)/2, Pi=(p-3)/2=h-2, H=p-1-kappa, k=3p-4-2H. Here k is ODD.
53:Let N_1,...,N_q be the nonempty rotation runs in cyclic order; M_i the reflection run between N_i,N_(i+1),
54:b_i=number of constant blocks of M_i>=1. Each internal run of M_i is genuinely empty, so adjacent block
55:values differ. Put x=R-q. P0 gives at least2p-R=p-2 odd nonempty runs, whence
56: 0<=x<=4, q=p+2-x, d=max|d(N_i)|<=x+1.                                                    (D1)
57:Indeed any N_i has at most R-(q-1)=x+1 letters. Write t=floor(x/2) in{0,1,2}.
58:T=sum z_i d(N_i) is ODD for every labelling, since sum r(N_i)=p+2 is odd. BAL gives |T|<=d,
59:hence |T|<=2t+1. No parity/run-purity strengthening is assumed.
60:
61:A pair is a two-consecutive-block arc inside one M_i; its two values differ. A singleton is one block.
62:Pair capacity c=sum floor(b_i/2)=(k-o)/2, where o=#{i:b_i odd}. Since k is odd, o is odd and o<=q.
63:Consequently, with u0=ceil(x/2),
64: c>=p-3-H+u0.                                                                           (D2)
65:If q is odd(x even), o<=q gives this bound; if q is even(x odd), o<=q-1 gives it.
66:Full pairing in an odd M_i leaves exactly one free block; in an even M_i it leaves none.
67:
68:## 2. Fresh window, direct GW2 and optional short segments
69:
70:For 2m>=2 disjoint singleton/pair arcs put s=p-m and E_+-=(R+-T)/2. With kappa=p-1-H,
71: gamma_m(E)=min(s,E+H-s,floor(E/2)).                                                       (W2)
72:If both gamma are nonnegative and Gamma(s,T):=gamma(E_+)+gamma(E_-) satisfies
73: Gamma(s,T)+G>=p-1, where G is the number of pair arcs, then an identity2p-subword exists.  (GW2)
74:Proof. Label arcs cyclically X_1,Y_1,...,X_m,Y_m. L2.1 yields proper patterns with exactly m intervals,
75:E+ independent of chosen endpoints, {E+,E-}={E_+,E_-} by L3.1, and u_P=(sum y_a-sum y_b)/2.
76:The attained u-set contains a CD sumset of singleton sets and G two-value sets, of size>=min(p,G+1).
77:The W interval contains0 and has Gamma+1 integers, hence min(p,Gamma+1) residues. If either count is p,
78:the sets meet; otherwise their sum>=p+1 by(GW2), so they meet. W gives the required subword. []
79:
80:For actual |T|=2j+1, 0<=j<=t, {E_+,E_-}={h+1+j,h-j}. Their floor-halves sum to h.
81:BOTH gamma equal their floor values whenever
82: alpha_t:=floor((h+1+t)/2) <= s <= beta_t:=H+ceil((h-t)/2).                                (F_t)
83:This checks the largest E for s>=floor(E/2), and the smallest E for s<=H+ceil(E/2), hence every intermediate
84:j as well. Directly beta_t-alpha_t=H-t for t0/1/2, by substituting h even/odd. These are NEW R2 windows.
85:In particular for T1, let
86: L_H:=p-H-ceil(h/2), U_H:=p-floor((h+1)/2).
87:Then every m in[L_H,U_H] has the same W interval of h+1 integers (both floor endpoints).
88:
89:Optional short-segment lemma (fresh). Reserve a segment of L consecutive blocks, L in{3,4,5}, disjoint
90:from a base arc system; its internal runs are empty. It may add0,2,...,2floor(L/2) singleton arcs, without
91:changing T or the IN/OUT labels of any nonempty N_i. Its additional u-values, with a fixed sign, form a
92:set of at least L residues, so its guaranteed gain is L-1 and m rises by at most floor(L/2).
93:Proof. Put delta_i=y_(i+1)-y_i!=0 within the segment. Every subset J of its L-1 successive edges is a union
94:of at most floor(L/2) separated intervals[a,b]; choose endpoint blocks a,b+1 for each. The added u-value
95:is, up to one fixed sign and halving, sum_(i in J)delta_i. All subset sums of the nonzero deltas occur,
96:so CD applied to {0,delta_i} gives at least min(p,L)=L residues. Inserting an even number of arcs inside
97:one empty-run region preserves both outer gap labels. Different reserved segments contribute independently
98:with their fixed signs; the L2.1 telescoping formula gives the asserted additional sumset. []
99:
100:Thus if a base with G pairs and reserved lengths L_a has T1, all m from m_b through m_b+sum floor(L_a/2)
101:lie in[L_H,U_H], and G+sum(L_a-1)>=Pi, an identity follows: the u-set has at least min(p,1+Pi) residues,
102:the fixed floor window h+1, and (Pi+1)+(h+1)=p+1. This is proved from W/L2.1/CD, not imported ARC+.
103:
104:## 3. Two fresh BAL constructions
105:
106:Flexible construction. Choose G0<=c disjoint pairs and2m0-G0>=0 singleton arcs on other blocks, possible
107:if G0+2m0<=k. Suppose k-2G0>0. Some M_i has a free block; realise the BAL change there by adding/deleting
108:a singleton, with NO pair loss. At its partner use the restricted e5 move: delete singleton, add singleton,
109:or split pair. Then G>=G0-1, arc count2m0+Delta with Delta in{-2,0,2}, and s' in{s0-1,s0,s0+1}.
110:If a pair IS lost, its split adds an arc; therefore Delta>=0 and s'<=s0. If every M_i has a free block
111:and all pair capacity is retained, both changes add/delete singletons and G is unchanged. Already balanced
112:T needs no move. Every use below retains at least2 arcs and consists only of singletons/pairs.
113:
114:Fixed-count construction. Suppose c>=G0+1, G0+2m0<=k-1 and2m0-G0>=1.
115:Choose M_l with at least2 blocks; designate its initial two blocks as one singleton and one reserved UNUSED
116:block. Removing these two lowers capacity by exactly1; choose G0 pairs among the rest and fill the other
117:2m0-G0-1 singletons outside those pairs and the reserved block. The stated block inequality ensures room.
118:Apply BAL at l. If the partner adds an arc, delete the designated singleton; if it deletes an arc, add the
119:reserved singleton. Both realise the parity flip by I7. Total arc count stays2m0 and at most ONE pair is
120:lost(at the partner), so G>=G0-1. No unsupported parity move on a longer arc occurs. []
121:
122:## 4. Fresh direct-parity filling and counting bounds
123:
124:After reserving short segments and fully pairing each remaining M_i, every odd-length remainder has ONE
125:free block (unused or singleton). A run with even remainder has none; call its edge RIGID. Let g_i be its
126:number of fixed pairs. For any signs z_i satisfying z_(i+1)=(-1)^(g_i)z_i at each rigid edge, set the free
127:bit a_i in{0,1} at every other edge to realise that same relation with g_i+a_i. The product around the cycle
128:is1, so G+xi is even, G=sum g_i, xi=sum a_i. This gives a genuine base of2m_b=G+xi arcs.
129:A fixed complete free-bit pattern determines z up to global sign; at most ONE of these two signs has T1.
130:This last observation bounds bad T1 labellings by the number of bad free-bit sets, without enumerating data.
131:
132:Sign-fill lemma. Let J be in{2,3,4}. Suppose r rigid edges, excess x=R-q, r+x<=J, and p+2-2J>J, r<q.
133:Then a compatible T1 labelling exists, in fact at least two. For J2,p>=11 there are at least C(p-2,3)
134:such labellings. (We use J2 at p7 only for the at-least-two conclusion.)
135:Proof. Rigid edges form a forest of paths. Fix signs within each nontrivial path component up to its global
136:orientation; include every non-singleton rotation vertex outside these paths as a separate component.
137:The signed contribution of a path has magnitude<=number of its vertices plus the total excess x, hence
138:<=r+1+x<=J+1; the same bound holds for an isolated deviant. Orient these components greedily against the
139:current sum to obtain total absolute value<=J+1, then flip all assigned signs if needed to make F>=0.
140:All remaining vertices are FREE SINGLETON rotations, n>=q-(2r+x)>=p+2-2J. Their contributions are freely+-1.
141:The required sum1-F has magnitude<=J<n and the parity of n, since total rotation mass is odd. It is
142:attainable in at least two ways. For J2,p>=11, n>=p-2>=9 and |1-F|<=2; the number of choices is at least
143:C(n,3)>=C(p-2,3), by the monotonicity of binomial coefficients up to the middle. []
144:
145:One-rigid-edge singleton count. If x0 and exactly ONE rigid edge, the number of compatible T1 labellings
146:is at least C(p,(p-1)/2): in contributions w_i=z_i d(N_i), the constrained pair is either equal or opposite.
147:In the equal case choose both+1 and let the other p signs sum to-1; in the opposite case choose+1,-1 and
148:let the other p signs sum to1. Both give that binomial lower bound.
149:For p>=7,
150: C(p,(p-1)/2)>=C(p,3)>1+C(p+1,2),                                                        (C1)
151:because C(p,3)-C(p+1,2)=p((p-3)^2-10)/6>=p>1.
152:For p>=11, putting n=p-2>=9 gives
153: C(p-2,3)>1+C(p+1,2).                                                                    (C2)
154:Indeed C(n+3,2)<=2C(n,2), since n^2-7n-6=(n-9)(n+2)+12>=0, while
155:C(n,3)>=(7/3)C(n,2) and C(n,2)/3>=12>1. These are symbolic counting inequalities, not enumeration.
156:
157:## 5. Middle-plus: kappa=h for EVERY p>=7
158:
159:Here H=h-2,k=2p-1=4h-3, and(D2) gives c>=Pi+ceil(x/2).
160:If c>=Pi+1 choose G0=Pi+1=h-1 and s0=alpha_t+1, except the p7,t1/t2 cases below.
161:For p>=11, h>=6 and beta_t-alpha_t=h-2-t>=2, so s0-1,s0,s0+1 all lie in(F_t).
162:The block budget is G0+2m0<=k iff2s0>=h, true by alpha_t>=h/2; and
163:2m0-G0=3h-1-2s0>=2h-6>=0. A free block exists(k-2G0=2h-1>0).
164:The flexible construction leaves G>=Pi and full floor sum h, so GW2 applies.
165:The same calculation works for p7,t0, where(F_0)=[2,4] and s03.
166:
167:If c=Pi, (D2) forces x0 and o=q=p+2: EVERY b_i is odd. Fully pair all c pairs and use s0=alpha_0+1.
168:Each M_i has one free block;2m0-Pi=3h-2s0 fits among q=2h+1 free blocks since2s0>=h-1.
169:The flexible construction costs NO pair. (F_0) has width h-2>=2, so all three s' have full floor windows,
170:and G=Pi suffices. No lower capacity occurs.
171:
172:p7,t1: use s04,G03,k13;2m06,3 singletons fit. The full-floor interval is[3,4]. A pair loss implies
173:s'<=4, hence s'3/4 and Gamma4 with G>=2. If s'5 both moves deleted arcs, so NO pair loss and G3;
174:at s5 both T1 and T3 have Gamma3 (respectively gamma pairs(2,1),(3,0)). In all cases Gamma+G>=6.
175:p7,t2: then x4,c>=4. Use fixed-count construction G03,s03,m04: G0+2m0=11<=k-1=12,
176:2m0-G0=5>=1. Its full-floor interval(F_2)={3}, so G>=2 and Gamma4 give6.
177:This proves D2-MP at its ENTIRE scope. []

179:## 6. Middle-minus: kappa=h-1 for EVERY p>=7
180:
181:Here H=h-1,k=2p-3=4h-5, and c>=Pi-1+ceil(x/2).
182:For c>=Pi+1 use G0=Pi+1,s0=alpha_t+1. All three s' are in(F_t) unless p7,t2:
183:H-t>=2 otherwise. Block budget holds since2s0>=h+2;2m0-G0>=2h-6>=0;
184:k-2G0=2h-3>0. Flexible BAL gives G>=Pi and full floor sum h.
185:p7,t2: use s04,G03,k11. (F_2)=[3,4]. If a pair is lost s'3/4 and Gamma4 suffice with G>=2.
186:If s'5 no pair is lost,G3, and Gamma>=3 for every T1/3/5: the gamma pairs are(2,2),(3,1),(3,0).
187:All are nonnegative and GW2 applies.
188:
189:For c=Pi, x<=2 and the number of even M_i is r=2-x. Fully pair every M_i; r+x=2, so the sign-fill lemma
190:at J2 gives at least two T1 labellings for every p>=7. There are f=q-r=p free bits. Write epsilon=h mod2.
191:G=Pi=h-2, xi=epsilon mod2,0<=xi<=p. The T1 floor m-window is
192: [floor(h/2), U_H]. It contains m=(h-2+xi)/2 provided xi>=2-epsilon;
193:the upper bound is automatic(xi<=2h-epsilon). When epsilon1, parity already gives xi>=1.
194:When epsilon0, avoid xi0: at most one T1 labelling has that free-bit pattern, and at least two exist.
195:Thus G=Pi and the T1 window give an identity.
196:
197:For c=Pi-1, (D2) forces x0 and all M_i odd. Since c=Pi-1>=1, some b_l>=3.
198:Reserve its initial3 blocks as an optional segment and fully pair all other remaining blocks.
199:G=c-1=Pi-2=h-4>=0, exactly one edge becomes rigid, and f=p+1=2h free bits remain.
200:The one-rigid-edge count(C1) exceeds1+C(p+1,2). Parity gives xi=epsilon mod2.
201:We need the base m_b and m_b+1 inside[floor(h/2),U_H], which is exactly
202: 4-epsilon<=xi<=2h-epsilon.
203:The upper bound is automatic from f and parity. If epsilon0 the bad choices are xi0/2, at most
204:1+C(p+1,2) free-bit sets; if epsilon1 only xi1 is bad, at most p+1 such sets. Each determines at most
205:one T1 labelling. Therefore a good labelling exists. The base has at least2 arcs, its optional segment
206:has gain2 and m-rise<=1; G+2=Pi. Section2's optional-segment kill applies.
207:All capacities are exhausted, proving D2-MM uniformly, including p7. []

209:## 7. Lower-middle endpoint: kappa=h-2 for EVERY prime p>=11
210:
211:Here H=h,k=2p-5=4h-7,h>=6 and c>=Pi-2+ceil(x/2).
212:If c>=Pi+1, take G0=Pi+1=h-1 and s0=alpha_t+2. (F_t) has width h-t>=4, so all s' stay in it.
213:The block budget is2s0>=h+4, true since2alpha_t>=h; and
214:2m0-G0=3h-1-2s0>=2h-8>=0, with k-2G0=2h-5>0. Flexible BAL gives G>=Pi and full floor sum h.
215:
216:It remains c in{Pi,Pi-1,Pi-2}. Put epsilon=h mod2 and
217: L=floor(h/2)-1, U=p-floor((h+1)/2).
218:This is the T1 floor m-window[L_H,U_H] for H=h. All short-segment bases below have m_b>=L>=2.
219:
220:c=Pi. Here o=k-2c=p-2 and r=q-o=4-x rigid edges after full pairing. Thus r+x=4;
221:the sign-fill lemma at J4 applies(p>=11), giving T1. There are f=q-r=p-2=2h-3 free bits.
222:G=Pi=h-2 and xi=epsilon mod2. Every such xi gives m=(h-2+xi)/2 in[L,U]:
223:the lower required bound is xi>=-epsilon, automatic; the upper is xi<=2h-epsilon, also automatic.
224:So GW2 with G=Pi applies directly, with arbitrary deviant rotation runs retained.
225:
226:c=Pi-1. Now x<=2, o=p, r=2-x and c=h-3>=3. Some M_i has b_i>=3: otherwise c=r<=2, impossible.
227:If an odd b_i>=3 exists, reserve its initial3 blocks. It costs1 pair, gives optional gain2 and m-rise<=1.
228:After full pairing the rest, G=Pi-2=h-4; that edge becomes rigid, so r'=3-x and f=q-r'=p-1=2h-2.
229:J3 sign-fill applies(p+2-6=p-4>3), giving at least two T1 labellings. The requirement
230: m_b,m_b+1 in[L,U]
231:is exactly2-epsilon<=xi<=2h-epsilon. The upper bound follows from f; parity gives xi=epsilon mod2.
232:When epsilon1 its lower bound is automatic. When epsilon0 only xi0 is bad; at most one T1 labelling has
233:that fixed free-bit pattern. Choose another. The optional segment kill has G+2=Pi.
234:
235:If no odd b_i>=3 exists, choose an even b_i>=4 and reserve its initial4 blocks. It costs2 pairs, gives
236:optional gain3 and m-rise<=2. The rigid count stays r'=2-x, f=p=2h-1, G=Pi-3=h-5.
237:J2 sign-fill and its strong count give at least C(p-2,3)>p compatible T1 labellings(C2).
238:The required range m_b,...,m_b+2 in[L,U] is
239: 3-epsilon<=xi<=2h-1-epsilon.
240:The upper bound is automatic from f and xi=h-5 modulo2. For epsilon0, only xi1 is bad(at most p patterns);
241:for epsilon1, only xi0 is bad. Thus a good T1 labelling exists, and G+3=Pi kills.
242:
243:c=Pi-2. Necessarily x0 and every b_i odd; c=h-4>=2. If one b_i>=5, reserve its initial5 blocks.
244:Otherwise every b_i is1 or3 and at least two are3; reserve those two distinct3-block runs.
245:Either choice costs2 pairs, gives TOTAL optional gain4 and total m-rise<=2. After pairing the rest,
246:G=Pi-4=h-6>=0, and r' is1 or2. The free-bit count f is p+1 or p. All rotation runs are singletons.
247:J2 sign-fill gives at least C(p-2,3)>1+C(p+1,2) T1 labellings(C2). We need
248: m_b,...,m_b+2 in[L,U], equivalently4-epsilon<=xi<=2h-epsilon.
249:The upper bound is automatic from f and parity xi=epsilon mod2. For epsilon0, bad xi0/2 account for at
250:most1+C(p+1,2) free-bit sets; for epsilon1, bad xi1 accounts for at most p+1. Each gives at most one T1
251:labelling, so a good one exists. The optional segment kill has G+4=Pi.
252:All capacities are exhausted; this proves D2-END at every p>=11. []

255:## 8. All FOUR top ends: H in{0,1,2,3}, EVERY prime p>=7
256:
257:This is kappa in{p-1,p-2,p-3,p-4}, with overlaps handled as one word class. For p7,H2 andH3 are respectively
258:MP(kappa4) andMM(kappa3), already proved. We now prove H0/H1 for all p and H2/H3 for p>=11.
259:Write u0=ceil(x/2),t=floor(x/2),alpha_t=floor((h+1+t)/2). The following table gives a fixed-count choice;
260:Gamma_min holds for EVERY actual odd T with |T|<=2t+1, and both gamma are nonnegative.

262:| H | t | s0 | G0 | Gamma_min |
263:|---|---|---|---|---|
264:| 0 | 0 | alpha_0 | h-1 | h |
265:| 0 | 1 | alpha_1 | h | h-1 |
266:| 0 | 2 | alpha_0 | h+1 | h-2 |

268:| 1 | 2 | alpha_2 | h | h-1 |
269:| 2 or3 (p>=11) | 0,1,2 | alpha_t | h-1 | h |

271:Window calculation in full. For H>=2, s0=alpha_t lies in(F_t), since H>=t; both gamma are full floors.
272:For H1,t<=1 the same reasoning applies. To check ALL other minima, write actual |T|=2j+1.
273:If h=2a+1(p=4a+1, hence a>=3), then alpha_0=alpha_1=a+1,alpha_2=a+2.
274:At H0,s=alpha_0, the gamma pairs(Emax,Emin) for j0,1,2 are
275: (a+1,a), (a+1,a-1), (a+1,a-2), respectively; their sums are h,h-1,h-2.
276:At H1,s=alpha_2 they are (a+1,a),(a+1,a-1),(a+2,a-2), summing h,h-1,h-1.
277:If h=2a+2(p=4a+3,a>=1), then alpha_0=a+1,alpha_1=alpha_2=a+2.
278:At H0,s=alpha_0 the pairs are (a+1,a+1),(a+1,a),(a+1,a-1), summing h,h-1,h-2.
279:At H0,s=alpha_1, for j0/1 the pairs are (a+1,a),(a+2,a-1), both summing h-1.
280:At H1,s=alpha_2 the pairs are (a+1,a+1),(a+2,a),(a+2,a-1), summing h,h,h-1.
281:These follow by literal substitution in(W2); all entries are>=0 even at p7(a1 in the second case).
282:They prove every row, including smaller j than the displayed worst t, without a sample-prime substitute.
283:
284:We check the fixed-count construction's hypotheses, not merely its gain.
285:(D2) says c>=2h-4-H+u0. For H0,t0 this is>=h=G0+1(h>=4); for t1,u0>=1 it is>=h+1;
286:for t2,u0=2 it is>=h+2. For H1,t2 it is>=2h-3>=h+1=G0+1.

288:Except for those two minima, c>=G0+1. In the exceptional parameters, if the actual c is any larger the
289:same fixed-count construction applies; only their minimum capacities need the separate cases below.
290:
291:The block budget G0+2m0<=k-1 is equivalent, row by row, to the following lower requirements on2s0:
292: 5-h;6-h;7-h;7-h;8-h;2H-h+5, respectively.

296:count construction is valid. It leaves G>=G0-1. Every row satisfies

## /Users/jinleic/repos/aifours/mathematics/evidence/six-lane-20261016/olson/proof-theoremP.md
SHA256 58a85ebeba8208e2f89b39a691235d2d1374656d8888ea905fe96aa75e1dafc2
LF 1-109
1:# Theorem P: a Chevalley–Warning proof of ordered EGZ for a class of dihedral words
2:
3:Status: written proof (lane MathOlson, 2026-10-16), mechanism checked by brute
4:force (`code/verify_marked.py`, `code/verify_theoremP.py`); NOT yet independently
5:reviewed.  This is a theorem about a CLASS OF WORDS in D_{2p}; it does not
6:prove ordered Olson for D_{2p} (the "many odd gaps" class remains open here).
7:
8:## Setting and conventions
9:
10:p is an odd prime. D_{2p} = {(v, e) : v in Z_p, e in {+1,-1}} with
11:(v, e)(v', e') = (v + e v', e e')  (rotations (x,+1), reflections (y,-1)).
12:For a word w = w_1 ... w_N and indices i_1 < ... < i_k the ordered product is
13:w_{i_1} w_{i_2} ... w_{i_k}.  An identity k-subword is such an index set with
14:product (0, +1).  Cyclic rotation of w preserves the existence of identity
15:k-subwords (a product U V becomes V U = U^{-1} (U V) U).
16:
17:Let w contain F >= 1 reflections.  Rotate w cyclically so that it ENDS with a
18:reflection:
19:    w = X_1 f_1 X_2 f_2 ... X_F f_F,
20:where f_t = (y_t, -1) are the reflections and X_t (t = 1..F) is the block of
21:rotations strictly between f_{t-1} and f_t (X_1 = rotations before f_1); n_t =
22:|X_t|.  ("Gap t" = X_t; cyclically these are exactly the gaps between
23:consecutive reflections.)
24:
25:## Theorem P
26:
27:If N = |w| <= 4p - 1 and  F + sum_{t=1}^{F} floor(n_t / 2) >= 3p - 1,  then w
28:has an identity 2p-subword.
29:
30:## Proof
31:
32:**Step 1 (parametrisation).** In each gap X_t list the rotations in index order
33:and group them into floor(n_t/2) consecutive PAIRS pi = (i, i'); if n_t is odd
34:the last rotation of X_t is a SINGLETON.  Introduce Boolean parameters
35:  u_2, ..., u_F  (gap colours; set u_1 := 0 and u_{F+1} := u_1 = 0),
36:  m_pi  for each pair pi  (pair marks).
37:Number of parameters:  M = (F - 1) + sum_t floor(n_t/2) >= 3p - 2.
38:
39:To a parameter point (u, m) assign the selection S(u, m):
40:* reflection f_t is selected iff u_t != u_{t+1}  (t = 1..F);
41:* both rotations of pair pi in gap t are selected iff m_pi XOR u_t = 1,
42:  otherwise neither;
43:* singletons are never selected.
44:
45:**Step 2 (injectivity; the empty selection).** The selection determines the
46:parameters: going left to right, u_1 = 0 and u_{t+1} = u_t XOR [f_t selected]
47:determine all u_t; then m_pi = [pi selected] XOR u_t.  Hence the map
48:(u, m) -> S(u, m) is injective, and S(u, m) is EMPTY iff (u, m) = 0: an empty
49:selection selects no reflection, so all u_t = u_1 = 0, and no pair, so all
50:m_pi = u_t = 0.
51:
52:**Step 3 (size).** |S(u,m)| = #{t : u_t != u_{t+1}} + 2 #{pi : m_pi XOR u_{t(pi)} = 1}.
53:The first term is the number of colour changes around the cycle 1..F, 1, hence
54:even.  So |S| is always EVEN, and |S| <= N <= 4p - 1.
55:
56:**Step 4 (signs and the product).** Let S = S(u,m).  For an element of S lying
57:in gap t or equal to f_t, the number of selected reflections strictly before
58:it is #{s < t : u_s != u_{s+1}}, which has the parity of [u_t != u_1] = u_t.
59:So in the ordered product every selected rotation x in gap t is multiplied by
60:c_t := (-1)^{u_t}, and a selected reflection f_t contributes c_t y_t to the
61:rotation coordinate.  The number of selected reflections is even (Step 3), so
62:the product is (V, +1) with
63:  V = sum_{t : u_t != u_{t+1}} c_t y_t + sum_{pairs pi selected} c_{t(pi)} x_pi,
64:where x_pi = x_i + x_i'.  Using (c_t - c_{t+1})/2 = u_{t+1} - u_t (which is
65:0 when u_t = u_{t+1} and c_t when they differ) and summation by parts with
66:u_1 = u_{F+1} = 0,
67:  sum_{t=1}^{F} (u_{t+1} - u_t) y_t = sum_{t=2}^{F} u_t (y_{t-1} - y_t).
68:For pairs: a selected pair (m XOR u_t = 1) contributes c_t x_pi; one checks
69:c_t x_pi [m XOR u_t] = x_pi m - x_pi u_t for both u_t = 0, 1.  Therefore, in Z_p,
70:  V(u, m) = sum_{t=2}^{F} beta_t u_t + sum_pi x_pi m_pi,
71:  beta_t = y_{t-1} - y_t - sum_{pi in gap t} x_pi.
72:V is a polynomial of degree 1 in the parameters (over F_p), and S(u,m) is an
73:identity subword iff V(u, m) = 0 in Z_p.
74:
75:**Step 5 (Chevalley–Warning on the Boolean box).** Write the size as the
76:integer polynomial
77:  Z(u, m) = sum_{t=1}^{F} (u_t + u_{t+1} - 2 u_t u_{t+1}) + 2 sum_pi (m_pi + u_{t(pi)} - 2 m_pi u_{t(pi)}),
78:which agrees with |S(u, m)| on {0,1}^M and has degree 2.  Over F_p put
79:  Q = (1 - V^{p-1}) (1 - Z^{p-1}),   deg Q <= 3(p - 1) < M.
80:On {0,1}^M, Q(x) = 1 if V(x) = 0 and Z(x) = 0 in F_p, and Q(x) = 0 otherwise
81:(Fermat).  Box lemma: for any polynomial Q over F_p of degree < M,
82:  sum_{x in {0,1}^M} (-1)^{|x|} Q(x) = 0.
83:(Proof: suffices for monomials; a monomial of degree < M omits some variable
84:x_j, and the involution flipping coordinate j pairs points with opposite signs
85:and equal values.)  Hence
86:  sum_{x : V(x) = 0, Z(x) = 0 mod p} (-1)^{|x|} = 0 in F_p.
87:The point x = 0 contributes +1, so there is a parameter point x != 0 with
88:V(x) = 0 and |S(x)| = Z(x) = 0 mod p.
89:
90:**Step 6 (conclusion).** By Step 2, S(x) is nonempty; by Step 3 its size is
91:even and at most 4p - 1; being divisible by p (odd), the size is a positive
92:even multiple of p below 4p, i.e. exactly 2p.  By Step 4 its ordered product is
93:the identity.  QED.
94:
95:## Remarks
96:
97:1. Corollary (reflection coset): any word of 3p-1 reflections in D_{2p} has an
98:   identity 2p-subword (F = 3p-1, all n_t = 0).  Finite-exact computations
99:   (m = 3..9) show 3m-1 is also the sharp threshold for composite m.
100:2. Which words are covered depends only on the R/F type pattern.  Uncovered:
101:   F - #(odd gaps) <= 2p - 2; this contains every extremal (length 4p-2)
102:   dihedral avoider found in the exhaustive D10 data (alternating odd blocks).
103:3. The 2-part (size parity) is handled ONLY through pairing inside gaps.  A
104:   general "mixed modulus" Chevalley–Warning statement that would also control
105:   parity is false (it would give D(G) = D*(G) for G = C_2^4 + C_6).
106:4. Prime powers m = p^k: the same argument with congruences mod p^k would need
107:   Brink's restricted-variable Chevalley theorem for prime-power moduli
108:   (Combinatorica 31 (2011) 127-130); its exact hypotheses have NOT been read
109:   from the source here, so the extension is CONDITIONAL and kept separate.

50:## 2. Arc systems (Lemma ARC) and optional segments (Lemmas ARC+, SEG)
51:
52:Arc: a nonempty set of consecutive blocks whose internal runs are all EMPTY; nu(X) := number of distinct values in X (>= 2 if
53:X has an internal run).  Arc system: 2m >= 2 pairwise disjoint arcs X_1, Y_1, ..., X_m, Y_m in cyclic order; the gap after
54:an arc (runs and blocks up to the next arc) contains the run right after the arc; gaps after X_i are IN, after Y_i OUT;
55:E+ := B + sum of d_t over IN-gap runs.
56:**Lemma 2.1.**  For a_i in X_i, b_i in Y_i, P := union of the runs R_(a_i + 1), ..., R_(b_i) is a proper pattern with exactly m
57:intervals, E+(P) = E+, and u_P = (1/2) sum_i (y_(a_i) - y_(b_i)).
58:Proof.  The i-th interval consists of internal (empty) runs of X_i after a_i, the IN-gap runs and internal runs of Y_i up to
59:b_i; OUT-gaps contain a run, so intervals are separated and P != [k]; empty runs have d = 0; theta telescopes.  []
60:A **segment** is a nonempty set Sigma of consecutive blocks inside one maximal reflection run (internal runs empty).  Given a
61:base arc system and pairwise disjoint segments Sigma_j disjoint from all base arcs, with integers t_j >= 0, a configuration
62:chooses blocks c_1 < ... < c_(2t'_j) in each Sigma_j (0 <= t'_j <= t_j) and adds them as single-block arcs.
63:**Lemma ARC+.**  (i) Every configuration is an arc system with 2(m_b + sum t'_j) arcs and the base value of E+.  (ii) Its
64:u-value is u_base + sum_j eps_j W_j, u_base in the base sumset {(1/2) sum_i (y_(a_i) - y_(b_i))}, eps_j = +-1 fixed by the

93:Let w avoid.  N_1, ..., N_q (q >= 1) are the nonempty runs in cyclic order, M_i the maximal reflection run between N_i and

95:choice puts c_i pairwise disjoint arcs inside M_i, sum c_i even; it induces z in {+-1}^q (up to sign) by z_(i+1) = -z_i iff c_i
96:is odd, and T := sum_j z_j d(N_j).
97:**Lemma 3.1.**  E+ = (R + T)/2 or (R - T)/2 (IN = the label +1 of z or of -z).  Proof: N_j lies in a gap; crossing M_i crosses
98:c_i arcs; IN runs give a_j to E+, OUT runs b_j.  []

100:ceil(E_min/2) >= kappa + 1 - m and floor(E_max/2) <= p - m (E_min, E_max the two values (R -+ |T|)/2), and total gain
101:(base sum of nu - 1 plus segment gains) at least Pi_need := p - ceil(R/2), then w has an identity 2p-subword.

104:parities of c_i and c_(i') gives |T| <= d (nothing to do if |T| <= d).
105:Proof.  Say T > d.  S(t) := sum_(j = i+1..i+t) z_j d(N_j), S(0) = 0, S(q) = T, steps <= d.  The least t with S(t) >= (T - d)/2
106:has 1 <= t <= q - 1 and S(t) < (T + d)/2; the change flips z on N_(i+1..i+t), giving T - 2S(t), of absolute value <= d.  []
107:Realisations of a parity change at M_j: delete a singleton arc of M_j; add a singleton on an unused block of M_j; or split a
108:two-block arc of M_j into two singletons.  Each changes the arc count by +-1 and loses at most one two-block arc.

6:
7:## Finding (confirmed)
8:
9:Pi_need < 0 can occur inside the hypotheses of Theorem SEG: an avoider has A <= 2p - 1 and, after the normalisation A >= B,
10:B <= kappa (K0star-r2 (Z0)), so R = A + B can exceed 2p when B >= 2 (e.g. parameters p = 11, kappa = 8, k = 12, R = 26 satisfy
11:R = 2p - k + 2 kappa, k >= p + 1, A <= 21 forces B >= 5 < kappa).  For such words the printed proof line
12:"sum t_j <= Pi_need" is false (the least admissible t_j are 0), and the printed (H1) m_hi - m_lo >= Pi_need + 1 can be <= 0,
13:whereas the proof needs the base value m_b in {m_lo, m_lo + 1} to satisfy m_b <= m_hi.  So the printed hypotheses do not
14:support the printed proof when Pi_need < 0.  The raw statement is therefore CORRECTED (narrowed, explicitly) as follows.
15:
16:## Correction
17:
18:Put Pi+ := max(0, Pi_need).  In Theorem SEG (v5 Sec. 5) read
19:   (H1') m_hi - m_lo >= Pi+ + 1   and   (H3') g_E >= Pi+ + 1
20:in place of (H1), (H3); in its proof read "sum t_j <= Pi+" and "m in [m_lo, m_lo + 1 + Pi+]".  With these hypotheses the proof
21:is unchanged: when Pi_need <= 0 all t_j = 0, every configuration is the base with m_b in {m_lo, m_lo + 1}, inside [m_lo, m_hi]
22:by (H1'), and Lemma 3.2 applies because the window sum floor(E+/2) + floor(E-/2) >= ceil(R/2) - 1 >= p - 1 - Pi_need >= p - 1
23:already (total gain >= 0 >= Pi_need).  (H3') is then automatic except for g_E = 0, where it is not needed either; it is kept
24:only to make the statement uniform.  For Pi_need >= 0, (H1') = (H1) and (H3') = (H3): nothing changes.
25:
26:## Effect on the corollaries (no change)
27:
28:Every application of Theorem SEG has Pi_need >= 1, so the correction does not touch them:
29:* SEG-p and MID: R = p, Pi_need = (p - 1)/2.
30:* SEG-num: R = p + delta with 5 delta <= p - 10, so R < 2p - 1 and Pi_need >= 1.
31:* SEG-k (separate addendum, not in this review set): if R >= 2p - 1 then k >= p + floor(R/2) + 1 >= 2p, hence
32:  R = 2p - k + 2 kappa <= 2 kappa <= 2p - 2, a contradiction; so R <= 2p - 2 and Pi_need >= 1 there.
33:K2P+, TOP and Lemmas ARC+, SEG, 3.2, BAL do not use the printed (H1)/(H3) of Theorem SEG.  K2P+ has R <= 2 kappa - 1 <= 2p - 3.

## /Users/jinleic/repos/aifours/mathematics/evidence/six-lane-20261016/olson/inverse-normalization-20261018/reflection/proof-K0star-r2.md
SHA256 6681676e4f80018e792c7117ce9fc524a31aeb397c35880f57468ada9c0396e1
LF 12-75
12:## 0. Setting
13:
14:D_(2p) = {(v,e)}, (v,e)(v',e') = (v + e v', e e'), rho_x = (x,+1), sigma_y = (y,-1).  A word is cyclic; an identity 2p-subword is
15:an increasing index set of size 2p with ordered product (0,+1); its existence is invariant under cyclic rotation
16:(proof-theoremP.md, Setting) and under the automorphisms rho_v -> rho_(cv), sigma_v -> sigma_(cv+d), c != 0.  An avoider has no
17:identity 2p-subword.
18:
19:**G-words.**  A G-word is a word of length N with F >= 1 reflections, R >= 1 rotations, all rotations in {rho_1, rho_-1}
20:(normalised x = 1).  [r2 fix 3: R >= 1 for all maximal-decomposition statements below; a word with R = 0 is one cyclic
21:reflection run (for k = 1 its sole "run" is empty with theta_1 = 0), it is handled by Lemma AR, and Lemmas GR0, H0 remain valid for
22:every representation, R = 0 included.]  Cutting every maximal cyclic reflection run at every change of letter gives the maximal constant blocks
23:S_1, ..., S_k (S_i = sigma_(y_i)^(l_i), l_i >= 1) and the rotation runs R_1, ..., R_k, R_i between S_(i-1) and S_i (indices mod k,
24:y_0 := y_k); R_i has a_i letters rho_1 and b_i letters rho_-1, r_i = a_i + b_i >= 0, d_i = a_i - b_i, theta_i := y_(i-1) - y_i.
25:R_i is an EMPTY run if r_i = 0; then y_(i-1) != y_i by maximality, i.e. theta_i != 0 (a *genuine* empty run).  g := number of
26:empty runs.  w is RGR (in RGR_N(p) of proof-DR.md) iff g = 0.  More generally a *representation* is any cyclic alternation
27:R_1 S_1 ... R_k S_k of constant blocks and possibly empty runs reproducing w (adjacent blocks may then have equal values).
28:Parameters (of the maximal decomposition unless a representation is named): A = sum a_i, B = sum b_i, R = A + B, D = A - B,
29:F = sum l_i, H = sum floor(l_i/2), kappa := p - 1 - H, o_F = #{i : l_i odd}, e_F = k - o_F, o_R = #{i : r_i odd},
30:e_R = k - o_R (EMPTY RUNS COUNT AS EVEN ROTATION RUNS).  For N = 4p - 2:  F = 2H + o_F and R = 2p - o_F + 2 kappa.        (0.1)
31:Patterns: P subset of [k] (a set of rotation runs, empty runs included); m(P) = number of maximal cyclic intervals of P; the
32:boundary blocks of P are the S_j with exactly one of R_j, R_(j+1) in P (2m(P) of them); beta(P) = number of even boundary blocks;
33:D_P = sum_(i in P) d_i; R_P = sum_(i in P) r_i; u_P := 2^(-1) sum_(i in P) theta_i in Z_p (additive; u_([k]) = 0; for a cyclic
34:interval P = {i, ..., j}: u_P = (y_(i-1) - y_j)/2, so u_P != 0 iff its two boundary blocks have different values).
35:Normalisation N: rho_v -> rho_(-v), sigma_v -> sigma_(-v) maps G-words to G-words, keeps every block length and every empty run
36:(and its genuineness), swaps A and B; so WLOG A >= B.
37:
38:## 1. Exact criteria (re-derived for representations with empty runs)
39:
40:**Lemma GR0.**  For any representation (a_i, b_i, l_i, y_i) of any length, with f_i = [z_i != z_(i+1)], Phi = sum f_i,
41:delta = #{i : l_i != f_i mod 2}, C_max = F - delta, E+ = sum_(z_i = +1) a_i + sum_(z_i = -1) b_i, E- = R - E+, s0 = 2p - C_max,
42:s1 = 2p - Phi, Ach(z) = 2Z cap [max(-s1, s0 - 2E-, -E-), min(s1, 2E+ - s0, E+)] and tau(z) = -2^(-1) sum_i z_i (y_i - y_(i-1)):
43:w has an identity 2p-subword iff some z in {+1,-1}^k has Ach(z) cap (tau(z) + pZ) != empty.
44:Proof (the proof of Lemma GR, proof-GR.md Sec. 2, re-checked line by line; it never uses r_i >= 1).  Cut w at the start of R_1.
45:A selection with an even number of reflections has zone signs z_1 = +1, z_(i+1) = z_i (-1)^(c_i), c_i = number of letters chosen
46:from S_i (so c_i = f_i mod 2, z defined also at empty runs, and z_(k+1) = z_1).  Multiplying from the left, a chosen rotation
47:(x,+1) adds e x and a chosen reflection (y,-1) adds e y and flips e (e = current sign).  Inside S_i the chosen letters all equal
48:sigma_(y_i), so they add z_i y_i if c_i is odd and 0 if c_i is even; the product is rho_V with V = sum_i z_i n_i +
49:sum_(f_i = 1) z_i y_i (n_i = signed sum of chosen rotations of R_i, 0 for an empty run), and summation by parts gives
50:sum_(f_i=1) z_i y_i = sum_i z_i (y_i - y_(i-1))/2, so V = t - tau(z) with t = A+ - A- (numbers of chosen effectively positive /
51:negative rotations).  The realisable totals C = sum c_i are exactly the even numbers in [Phi, C_max] (each c_i independently runs
52:over f_i, f_i + 2, ..., l_i - [l_i != f_i mod 2] >= f_i), and for even t a pair (A+, A-) with A+ + A- = 2p - C, A+ <= E+,
53:A- <= E- exists for some admissible C iff t is in Ach(z) (the computation of proof-GR.md Sec. 2, unchanged).  Every admissible
54:data point is realised by a selection; global sign change z -> -z maps (Ach, tau) to (-Ach, -tau).  []
55:**Lemma H0 (half windows).**  For a representation and a proper pattern P put
56:  lambda(P) := min(p - m + 1, D_P + B - kappa + m - beta, floor((D_P + B)/2) + 1)   (m = m(P), beta = beta(P), kappa = p - 1 - H),
57:  I(P) := [1 - lambda(P^c), lambda(P) - 1]  (integers; possibly empty),
58:  I(empty) := [-min(p, A - kappa - 1, floor(A/2)), min(p, B - kappa - 1, floor(B/2))].
59:Then w avoids iff u_P notin I(P) (mod p) for every proper P and 0 notin I(empty) (mod p).
60:Proof.  Lemma GR0 with P = {z_i = +1}.  As sum theta_i = 0, tau(z_P) = 2^(-1) sum_i z_i theta_i = sum_(i in P) theta_i = 2 u_P,
61:and 2 is invertible, so the condition for z_P is u_P notin [ceil(lo/2), floor(hi/2)] (mod p).  For P proper with Phi = 2m and
62:beta even boundary blocks, delta = beta + (o_F - (2m - beta)), so c := C_max/2 = H + m - beta; E+ = A_P + B_(P^c) = D_P + B;
63:hence floor(hi/2) = min(p - m, E+ + c - p, floor(E+/2)) = lambda(P) - 1, and E+(P^c) = E-(P), same m, beta, gives
64:ceil(lo/2) = 1 - lambda(P^c).  P = empty (z = all -1): Phi = 0, c = H, E+ = B, E- = A gives I(empty); P = [k] gives -I(empty).
65:No step uses the length or r_i >= 1.  []
66:**Facts for G-avoiders of length 4p - 2** (F >= 1; if R = 0, Lemma AR of c2/invstar/proof-GR0-r1.md: 2p - 1 adjacent reflection
67:pairs and EGZ; so R >= 1; a G-word with g >= 1 has k >= 2, F >= 2):
68: (Z0) A, B <= 2p - 1 (2p equal rotations); after N, B <= kappa (if A, B >= kappa + 1 then 0 is in I(empty)); hence by (0.1)
69:      o_F >= 2 kappa - B + 1 (R = A + B <= 2p - 1 + B).  (Pairs) kappa >= 0 (p disjoint pairs sigma_y sigma_y inside blocks, each
70:      pair adjacent in the selection, give 1).  (V) kappa <= o_F - 1 (Davenport-alpha, proof-dihedral-partial.md Sec. 4, with
71:      the H gaps "every second gap inside each block", pairwise non-adjacent; the gap at an empty run is not used; F >= 2,
72:      N = 4p - 2 < 4p).
73: (P0) e_R <= e_F + 2 kappa.  Proof: Theorem P (proof-theoremP.md; its gaps are the F gaps between consecutive reflections,
74:      n_t = r_i at the gap of R_i and 0 elsewhere, empty runs included) gives F + sum_i floor(r_i/2) <= 3p - 2, i.e.
75:      R + o_R >= 2p; with (0.1) this is o_R >= o_F - 2 kappa.  []

## /Users/jinleic/repos/aifours/mathematics/evidence/six-lane-20261016/olson/inverse-normalization-20261018/rotation/notes-GRX-v1.md

1:# Lemma GRX: exact solvability criterion for EVERY word over D_(2p) (OB1)
2:
3:Owner OlsonRotationRigidity.  Written after host clock 2026-10-18T01:52:10-07:00.  Status [W] unreviewed; the
4:statement is a direct generalisation of Lemma GR (olson/proof-GR.md Sec. 2, reviewed) and Lemma GR0
5:(c2/invstar/proof-GR0-r1.md, unreviewed); the proof below is self-contained.  Controls [F] in Sec. 3.
6:
7:## 1. Representation and statement
8:
9:p odd prime, conventions of olson/proof-dihedral-partial.md Sec. 0.  Let w have F >= 1 reflections.  Read w
10:cyclically and cut every maximal cyclic reflection run at each change of value: w is a cyclic alternation
11:    R_1 S_1 R_2 S_2 ... R_k S_k,   S_i = sigma_(y_i)^(l_i) (l_i >= 1),   R_i = rotations with value multiset X_i,
12:|X_i| = r_i >= 0 (R_i may be EMPTY; then y_(i-1) != y_i when the blocks are maximal, but the criterion below holds
13:for every such representation, maximal or not).  y_0 := y_k, theta_i := y_(i-1) - y_i, sum theta_i = 0.
14:For z in {+-1}^k: f_i = [z_i != z_(i+1)] (z_(k+1) = z_1), Phi = sum f_i (even), delta = #{i : l_i != f_i mod 2},
15:C_max = F - delta, X^z := multiset union over i of z_i X_i (each value multiplied by z_i), R = sum r_i,
16:T(z) := sum_{i : z_i = +1} theta_i (mod p), and Sigma_m(Y) := set of sums of size-m sub-multisets of Y.
17:
18:**Lemma GRX.**  w has an identity 2p-subword iff there are z and an even C with Phi <= C <= C_max, 0 <= 2p - C <= R
19:and T(z) in Sigma_(2p-C)(X^z).
20:(F = 0: identity 2p-subword iff 0 in Sigma_(2p)(X); rotations commute.  The sign pattern may be normalised z_1 = +1.)
21:
22:## 2. Proof
23:
24:A selection with an even number of reflections is given by (i) z_i := (-1)^(number of selected reflections
25:cyclically before R_i, counted from a fixed cut point at the end of S_k), (ii) c_i := number of selected letters of
26:S_i, with c_i = f_i (mod 2) [the parity of selected reflections in S_i is exactly what changes the zone sign between
27:R_i and R_(i+1)], (iii) any sub-multiset of each X_i.  Conversely each such datum with 0 <= c_i <= l_i is realised
28:(choose any c_i letters of the constant block S_i; any chosen rotations).  Because S_i is constant, its c_i selected
29:letters multiply to sigma_(y_i) (c_i odd) or 1 (c_i even); so the ordered product, read from the cut, is
30:    rho_V,  V = sum_i z_i (sum of chosen values in X_i) + sum_{i : f_i = 1} z_i y_i      (mod p),
31:by induction along the word (a rotation rho_x in a zone of sign z contributes z x; a reflection sigma_y entered in
32:a zone of sign z contributes z y and flips the zone sign; the even total makes the product a rotation).
33:Summation by parts: sum_{f_i=1} z_i y_i = sum_i y_i (z_i - z_(i+1))/2 = sum_i z_i (y_i - y_(i-1))/2 = -(sum_i z_i theta_i)/2,
34:and sum_i z_i theta_i = 2 sum_{z_i=+1} theta_i - sum_i theta_i = 2 T(z).  Hence V = sigma - T(z), sigma := the sum of
35:the chosen elements of X^z.  The size is C + m with C = sum c_i, m = number of chosen rotations.  For fixed z the
36:realisable C are exactly the even numbers in [Phi, C_max]: c_i ranges over the integers in [0, l_i] congruent to
37:f_i mod 2, i.e. from f_i up to l_i - [l_i != f_i mod 2]; summing, C ranges over all values of the right parity
38:between Phi and F - delta (each c_i moves in steps of 2), and Phi + C_max = F - delta + Phi is even because F = sum l_i
39:= delta + Phi (mod 2).  The rotations are chosen independently of the c_i.  So the selection is an identity
40:2p-subword iff C + m = 2p and sigma = T(z), as claimed.  The global sign change z -> -z maps X^z -> -X^z and
41:T(z) -> sum_{z_i=-1} theta_i = -T(z), so z_1 = +1 is a normalisation.  Identity subwords are invariant under cyclic
42:rotation of w (UV = 1 iff VU = 1), so the cyclic reading is exact.  []
43:
44:Specialisations: all rotation values in {1, -1} gives Lemma GR (Sigma_m(X^z) is then the window
45:{m - 2q : max(0, m - E+) <= q <= min(m, E-)}, E+- as in GR), and with empty runs Lemma GR0.
46:
47:## 3. Controls [F] (code/grx.py, code/rawdp.py, code/test_grx_v1.py; output tmp/test_grx_v1.out.json)
48:

50:3,730 exhaustive D10 avoiders (N = 18; all NO); 3,738 RGR avoiders p = 7, N = 26 (all NO); 16 positive family
51:words p in {5,7,11,13} (all NO); 608 negative controls (positive controls with an explicit random 2p-index set forced
52:to multiply to the identity; all YES); 1,800 random words p = 5, 7, N = 4p-3..4p-1 (uniform and near-rigid with one
53:random rotation); 313 deletions and 287 one-letter substitutions of known avoiders.  0 disagreements, 0 unexpected
54:values.  (Wall 3.0 s, 01:52:07-01:52:10 PDT.)  Hash pins are in the closing manifest.

## /Users/jinleic/repos/aifours/mathematics/evidence/six-lane-20261018-96h/olson/continuations/reflection-round2/proof-Rp-integer-lift-v1.md
SHA256 34cb5e007095b6d3c9c9eb91b2385c9e52cd94f78cd5b82c73bac8a31961df69
LF 80-114
80:## 3. Connectivity of the pattern graph (proved here)
81:
82:For 1 <= m with 2m < k, let F_m be the proper nonempty subsets of the k-cycle with exactly m cyclic intervals; join two
83:patterns by an edge when they differ at exactly one run index and both belong to F_m.
84:
85:**Lemma CONNECT.**  The graph F_m is connected.  Every run index i is toggled by at least one edge of F_m.
86:Proof.  A pattern has exactly 2m boundaries between adjacent run indices, lying at distinct block sites of the k-cycle;
87:these boundaries alternate outside-to-inside and inside-to-outside.  Moving a boundary one site into a free adjacent site
88:without colliding with another boundary toggles exactly one run and leaves the interval count m unchanged.
89:We prove that any two such boundary configurations with the alternating labels are linked by these moves, including a
90:pattern and its complement.
91:
92:Put n=2m.  Mark one outside-to-inside boundary as number 1; in cyclic order mark the other boundaries 2,...,n.  Choose
93:integer lifts of their positions satisfying
94:   x_1 < x_2 < ... < x_n < x_1 + k.
95:Labels alternate with the number.  Every pattern can be represented this way, with boundary 1 chosen at any of its
96:outside-to-inside boundaries.  The feasible lifted configurations form
97:   L = {x in Z^n : x_(i+1)-x_i >= 1 for 1<=i<n, x_1+k-x_n >= 1}.
98:A feasible unit change of one coordinate projects to the allowed boundary move above.  We show L is connected.
99:
100:Given x,y in L, z_i := min(x_i,y_i) also lies in L.  For consecutive coordinates this follows from
101:min(x_(i+1),y_(i+1)) >= min(x_i+1,y_i+1) = z_i+1.  For the wrapping inequality, if z_1=x_1, then z_n<=x_n gives
102:z_1+k-z_n>=x_1+k-x_n>=1; the other case is the same.
103:If x != z, put S={i:x_i>z_i}.  A coordinate i in S can be decreased by one whenever its preceding cyclic gap is >1;
104:this preserves the preceding gap, and enlarges the following one.  If that preceding gap equals 1, its predecessor is
105:also in S: writing x_0=x_n-k and z_0=z_n-k, x_i=x_(i-1)+1 and z_i>=z_(i-1)+1 together with x_i>z_i imply
106:x_(i-1)>z_(i-1).  If no coordinate of S were decreasable, following predecessors would force every coordinate into S and
107:every gap to equal 1.  Their sum would give k=n, contrary to 2m<k.  A decreasable coordinate therefore exists; repeated
108:steps reduce sum_i(x_i-z_i) until x=z.  Likewise y can be decreased to z, giving a unit-move path x to y by reversing
109:that second path.  Thus L, and hence F_m, is connected.  For the complement of a pattern choose boundary 1 at one of its
110:old inside-to-outside boundaries; the same argument applies (no claim that the old labels remain at their old sites).
111:
112:There is at least one allowed boundary move because n<k leaves a free site adjacent to some boundary.  It toggles some
113:index t.  Rotate the whole configuration by i-t to obtain an edge toggling any prescribed i.  []
114:

## /Users/jinleic/repos/aifours/mathematics/evidence/six-lane-20261018-96h/olson/continuations/reflection-round2/proof-Rp-integer-lift-v1.md
SHA256 34cb5e007095b6d3c9c9eb91b2385c9e52cd94f78cd5b82c73bac8a31961df69
LF 140-148
140:## 5. A cyclic signed-sum lemma
141:
142:**Lemma LIGHT.**  Let n nonnegative weights be arranged on a cycle, with n>=2f, f>=1.  There is an independent set of f
143:vertices (no two cyclically adjacent) of weight at most f/n times the total weight.  The same conclusion holds for a
144:disjoint union of paths with n vertices (use the vertices in their path order).
145:Proof.  The set {0,2,...,2(f-1)} is independent on the n-cycle: its wrapping gap is n-2(f-1)>=2.  Its n cyclic translates
146:all have f vertices and each vertex belongs to exactly f translates.  Their average weight is f/n times the total, so
147:one is no heavier than that.  For a disjoint union of paths, list the paths consecutively and add edges to turn this list
148:into one cycle; independence in the augmented cycle implies independence in the original paths.  []

This endpoint exists exactly under D2. It can exist when the SEG-num interval is empty (p=31, delta=4, endpoint 11, SEG-num interval [12,11]). K:34-41 is superseded as a coverage description by those exact formulas; no broader band or realizability claim follows.
14:
15:## T-1: T:59-60, a sufficient rectangle bound, not an attained box minimum
16:
17:Replace the assertion that the minimum of 2g-b occurs at (1,B0), with its claimed equivalence, by: on the entire allowed box, 2g-b >= 2-B0. Therefore A0+floor(D0/2) <= p+1 suffices for the second SEG-k inequality. If B0=0 the corner (1,0) is excluded by g+b>=2; no attained-minimum or necessary-condition claim is made. T2's sufficient hypothesis is unchanged.
18:
19:## B-1: B:27,30 and BP:17, justify d >= 1
20:
21:Within each stated range used by B.2 or B-prime, p-delta >= 1. P0 forces at least k-2kappa = p-delta odd rotation runs. An odd run is nonempty and its imbalance a_j-b_j is an odd integer, hence has absolute value at least 1 even if the run is mixed. Thus d = max |a_j-b_j| >= 1. Supply this argument before B.2(v) and (vii), and again when BP rechecks the required B.2 bounds rather than invoking B.2 outside its range.
22:
23:## B-2: B:67,71-76 and BP:24-25, zero remaining gain
24:
25:The correct unconditional estimate is G_need <= max(0, Pi_need-a-lambda+2). If G_need=0, choose all t_j=0; the already established width m_hi>=m_lo+1 gives B.3 immediately. Apply the chains bounding ceil(G_need/2) and G_need-W2/2 in B:71-76 and BP:24-25 only in the remaining case G_need>0, where Pi_need-a-lambda+2 is positive. In that case the printed chains are valid. This is also the reading already specified by the v2 package section 4.5 and the Codex review; no negative upper bound on a zero G_need is asserted.

7:## Exact setting and accepted scope
8:Let p≥5 be prime, and let w be a word of length4p−2 over D_2p. Its R≥1 rotations are all equal to one nonidentity rotation, normalized to ρ1 (B=0); F≥1. Every maximal constant reflection block has odd length, and empty intervening rotation runs must be genuine (the adjacent reflection values differ). Let k be the number of these blocks, H=(F−k)/2, κ=p−1−H≥1, and Δ=k−p−κ−1=p+κ−1−R.
9:
10:For a proper cyclic interval S=[a,b] of runs let O(S)=[b+1,a−1], and m be rotation mass. S is heavy if m(S)≥2Δ−1. It is admissible if m(O(S))≥2Δ−1 and either O(S) has one run, or it has a run x other than its last run for which both m([b+1,x+1]) and m([x,a−1]) are at least2Δ−1. Prefixes/suffixes here are in the linear order of O(S), not a second traversal of the cycle.
11:
12:- **B0S-CJ:** if R≥4κ+1 and either Δ≤0 or there are two disjoint heavy admissible intervals, w has an increasing-index identity2p-subword.
13:- **Strengthened B0S-CJ triple-prime:** the same conclusion holds under R≥4κ−4 **and** 2κ≤p−1. Both conditions are retained.
14:- **Corollary1, corrected:** a partition of the cycle into three **nonempty** consecutive arcs, each of mass≥2Δ−1, supplies two disjoint heavy admissible intervals.
15:- **Corollary2:** under the respective R hypothesis, there is no avoider if Δ≤1 or p≥κ+5Δ−5. The sufficient shortcut is 4Δ≤2κ+7 under R≥4κ+1, but only 4Δ≤2κ+2 under the strengthened hypothesis. The p≤23 conclusion of the original corollary does **not** transfer wholesale to the strengthened version.
16:
17:These statements apply to the specified B=0 setting only. RES_<(p) consequences import its accepted normalization into that setting. They do not normalize arbitrary rotations.

27:## Mandatory corrections and disposition
28:Read the immutable texts through this overlay; no certificate or scientific output is changed.
29:
30:1. **Original Corollary2, p≤23.** Replace the parameter-enumeration justification by this written argument. A violating triple satisfies 3κ+2+Δ≤p≤κ+5Δ−6, so4Δ≥2κ+8. With Δ≤κ−1, this forces κ≥6. Ifκ=6 then Δ=5 and p=25, not prime. Ifκ≥7 then Δ≥6 and p≥29. Thus no such triple has prime p≤23. RES_<(p)'s accepted B=0 setting is an explicit import; k≤p falls into the Δ≤1 branch.
31:2. **Corollary1.** Insert “nonempty” before “consecutive arcs.” For Δ≥1 positivity already forces nonemptiness; the explicit word is needed in the degenerate Δ≤0 statement.
32:3. **Strengthened proof, greedy step.** Apply 2|P′|+2≤Φ(P′)≤2κ to P′, yielding |P′|≤κ−1 and hence |P|≤κ after adding one run. Properness also follows from P⊆Z⊊[k]. The omitted possibility of the added run bordering the same interval on both sides would make P=[k], so P⊆Z excludes it. Thus the +1/0/−1 interval-count trichotomy used by the increment lemma is complete.
33:4. **Strengthened Δ≤0 branch.** Use item5's SIGMA-2P argument followed by item6's rechecked constructive contradiction, not an invocation of the original B0S++ theorem with its stronger R hypothesis. Item7's import list includes those two steps as well as C1/C2/F1/F2.
34:5. **Remaining notation/status notes.** In CJ's x* argument, “[x*+2,a−1]” means the part of O after x*+1 (empty at the endpoint). For the (29,7,6) remark, the |O|=1 case is impossible since its mass≤1<11. The k=1 example precedes the trivial R≥2p cut and illustrates the common Δ≤0 branch only. Strengthened r4 references to CJ-r1 are read through CJ-r2's errata. Historical “r3/UNREVIEWED” headers do not override the r4 pin and this verdict. The parity observation δ_P≤R is sufficient for L3; no stronger assertion is required.
